L-188 — Exact DTW completion moved to a compiled batch kernel

Bottleneck removed: Combined-component completion previously performed channel assembly, pair scaling, allocation and a compiled DTW call separately for every candidate. The new batch boundary packs each symbol group's four 64-sample channels once and completes all candidates in one no-GIL compiled loop while preserving distance-v1 scaling, stable predecessor order and path-length normalization.

Correctness and speed: Focused component and combined-search exhaustive-oracle tests pass, including partial/absent channel coverage. A deterministic 1,040-row benchmark measured 9.832 seconds for scalar completion and 0.042 seconds for the batch kernel (232.2×), with maximum absolute deviation 2.22e-16 and no ranking change. The complete repository test suite also passes.

Boundary: This changes only exact distance computation after a candidate is admitted. Proposal generation and strict next-bound closure are unchanged, and no outcome or label was read.

L-189 — Price-component packed bound no longer computes unrelated channels

Optimization: Price-only proposal and threshold scans now evaluate exactly the six distance-v1 price channels. They no longer unpack or compute coarse, stage, structural, candle/volatility, volume/shock or market-context bounds that cannot affect price-component ranking.

Parity and speed: The specialized branch-aware result is bit-for-bit equal to the full kernel's price component in synthetic family coverage and a real 65,536-row packed slice. That real slice improved from 1.521 to 0.395 seconds (3.85×). Focused search tests and the complete repository suite pass.

Boundary: Composite retrieval still uses every component. This specialization changes neither eligibility nor the price lower-bound formula and opens no outcome or label.

L-190 — Exact staged price-plus-DTW search closes the real authority 6.67× faster

Certified cascade: The staged search exactly completes a stable 16,384-row rigid-price-bound seed to establish a finite top-20 upper bound, retains every eligible rigid bound equal to or below it, evaluates the stronger DTW bound only for those rows, and exactly completes every combined bound equal to or below it. Only strict-greater-than rows are pruned, preserving ties and distinct-symbol selection.

Specialized reconstruction: Component completion now materializes only the six 48-sample rigid-price channels and four 64-sample DTW channels it consumes. Unit coverage proves these projections, rigid distances and completed price distances are bit-identical to full distance-v1 representations.

Real authority parity: On the frozen early case, all 20 episode IDs, ordering and hexadecimal distances exactly match the prior scalar combined result. The cascade classified 1,961,577 eligible rows, admitted 638,971 past rigid price, admitted 72,531 past combined DTW and exactly completed 72,646 rows including the seed. End-to-end time fell from 941.3 to 141.1 seconds (6.67× overall); against the first staged implementation it fell from 218.4 to 141.1 seconds. The complete repository suite passes.

Profile: Rigid and DTW bound phases now take 8.0 and 12.1 seconds. Seed and final exact reconstruction take 63.6 and 56.4 seconds, identifying cross-query source reuse as the next optimization target.

Boundary: The run used only historical chart prefixes and independently verified packed/DTW stores. No subsequent return, outcome, label or final-period result was opened.

L-191 — Explicit inventory cache prepared for multi-query retrieval

Design: Batch mode can now wrap any OHLCV source in a thread-safe LRU or inventory-sized cache, preload distinct instruments concurrently and borrow cached canonical frames through an explicit read-only interface. Default directory-source behavior remains the existing 32-symbol cache.

Observed tradeoff: Preloading all 12,080 NASDAQ frames took 73.0 seconds. A subsequent frozen early-case retrieval remained hexadecimal-exact and took 121.6 seconds versus 141.1 seconds without the inventory cache, at 12.6 GiB observed peak RSS on the currently exposed 31-GiB/8-CPU environment. The cache recorded 12,080 misses and 2,374 retrieval hits.

Further reduction: Exact price projections now share one empty immutable channel placeholder instead of constructing one pandas frame per candidate and borrow cached frames without defensive copies. A 1,000-window specialized projection takes 0.359 seconds. Focused cache/isolation tests and the complete repository suite pass.

Boundary: The cache is an execution optimization over immutable historical chart prefixes. It does not cache or expose outcomes, labels or future rows.

L-192 — Same-month combined-retrieval concurrency POC prepared

Scope: Four frozen March 2014 development queries spanning four registry liquidity cells will run concurrently against one preloaded canonical source cache. Each query receives two of the eight exposed CPUs for rigid bounds, DTW bounds and exact completion; output order remains registry order.

Acceptance: All four staged certificates must complete with zero process swap and no outcome/label access. The included OHAI authority must reproduce all 20 frozen scalar episode IDs, symbols, cutoffs and hexadecimal distances exactly. Timing, cache state and peak RSS are observational and excluded from semantic case digests.

Decision boundary: This POC selects the safe concurrency policy for the durable 3,936-query producer. It cannot authorize outcome evaluation or production promotion.

L-193 — Four-query outer concurrency rejected as the production default

Frozen execution: The preregistered March 2014 POC completed four simultaneous queries with two inner threads each against one preloaded 12,080-symbol cache. All four certificates closed, the frozen OHAI authority reproduced all 20 episode IDs and hexadecimal distances exactly, process swap remained zero and neither outcomes nor labels were opened.

Measured result: Inventory preload took 74.8 seconds and the concurrent query phase took 408.8 seconds. Individual queries took 150.3–408.4 seconds and observed peak RSS reached 38,402.9 MiB. The immutable result digest is fb2628d6…b479.

Decision: Four outer queries competing for two threads each provide insufficient throughput benefit for their memory and per-query latency cost. This tested configuration is retained as evidence but rejected as the durable producer default; sequential queries may instead reuse prepared data while each query uses all exposed compute.

L-194 — Exact symbol preparation is reusable across sequential queries

Optimization: Exact completion now converts each immutable symbol to timestamp, OHLCV and benchmark-aligned NumPy arrays once and accepts an explicit batch-lifetime prepared-symbol cache. Later queries reuse those arrays while independently reconstructing their requested windows. Cached entries retain and recheck both stock and benchmark causal-prefix bindings; the generic source interface remains supported.

Exactness and speed: Two consecutive frozen OHAI searches produced the same authority-exact result digest 7af4efe6…7f46. The first took 127.8 seconds and the reused query took 69.8 seconds. A warm exact seed ladder kept all 20 results hexadecimal-exact; 2,048 seed rows was fastest at 62.7 seconds versus 62.9 for 512, 66.6 for 8,192 and 70.4 for 16,384.

Verification: A repeated-query regression proves equal matches and no candidate-symbol source reloads after preparation. Focused cache, exact-batch and combined-search tests pass 29/29; the complete repository suite passes 989/989 and git diff --check is clean.

L-195 — Progressive exact completion tested and rejected

Hypothesis: Process combined-bound candidates in increasing batches, recompute the distinct-symbol threshold after each batch and skip later candidates above the tighter threshold.

Measured rejection: The frozen OHAI result remained hexadecimal-exact, but elapsed time regressed to 291.5 seconds. Only 529 same-symbol rows and 1,318 globally bounded rows were avoided, while repeated per-symbol projection across batches dominated execution. The experiment therefore failed its performance purpose.

Disposition: The progressive implementation was fully removed. Exact completion remains one grouped pass over every candidate at or below the certified seed threshold. This negative result is retained here to prevent an unsupported retry.

L-196 — Durable all-query combined retrieval producer prepared

Execution contract: The 3,936 frozen registry queries run sequentially, each using all eight exposed CPUs, one inventory-sized immutable source cache and one batch-lifetime prepared numeric-array cache. The exact seed is frozen at 2,048 rows from the authority-exact timing ladder. Each completed query is published as a create-only sealed receipt and progress is atomically replaced after every query, so interruption can lose only the active query.

Correctness boundaries: Resume independently validates each case seal, query/fold/upstream identities, certificate count relations, strict rigid and combined bound closure, exact distinct-symbol ordering, final threshold, and a reconstructed staged-search result digest. Packed resident and DTW file identities are rechecked after every newly computed case; any identity drift or process swap refuses publication. Runtime dependencies include no outcome or label module.

Pre-scale evidence: Ten dedicated contract/refusal tests plus the staged-search tests pass. The receipt validator also accepts the real frozen OHAI certificate and independently reconstructs authority result digest 7af4efe6…7f46. The complete repository suite passes 999/999 and the HTML/diff checks are clean. H0/H1 freezing remains required before launch.

L-197 — Independent all-query combined verifier prepared

Complete audit: The verifier independently validates every producer/case seal, reconstructs all staged certificate result digests, normalizes the physically distinct main and overflow store layouts, resolves all 78,720 analogue episode IDs, and checks symbol, cutoff, quality tier, causal gap and strict same-symbol non-overlap against the immutable packed inventory. Aggregate case-file hashes, semantic digests and eligible-count extrema must reproduce the terminal result.

Independent inputs and reruns: Full packed and DTW generation content is rehashed and record-validated. The previously frozen scalar OHAI authority must match exactly. One deterministic query from each of the six folds is then reconstructed and rerun with the preregistered algorithm, requiring exact matches and certificate result digests. No outcome or label path is accepted.

Pre-run verification: Five focused verifier tests cover deterministic fold sampling, independent episode lookup, physical-layout normalization, seal mutation and certificate sensitivity; all focused producer/verifier tests pass 15/15. The complete repository suite passes 1,004/1,004 under concurrent producer load. The verifier can execute only after all 3,936 producer receipts are terminal.

L-198 — Bit-exact compiled resampling and direct-array completion accepted

Alternatives tested: Compiling both interpolation grids and interpolation changed sampled values by as much as 1.71e-13 and was rejected. Passing NumPy's original source/target grids into the compiled loop is bit-exact across lookbacks, output sizes, complete rows and missing-data rows. A first integration attempt used Python bool as a compiled allocation dtype; Numba refused it before any numeric test ran. Changing only that dtype to np.bool_ closed compilation.

Accepted design: Exact price reconstruction now returns dense sampled/presence arrays. Rigid and DTW completion consume those arrays directly instead of allocating tens of thousands of temporary dictionaries and Representation objects and immediately unpacking them. Existing object APIs remain available and are differentially equal to the direct path.

Exactness and performance: All 30 focused exact-batch/staged-search tests pass. A 50,000-row missing-data interpolation gate is bit-for-bit equal and 3.64× faster. Frozen OHAI cold/warm searches are authority-exact with the same deterministic 2,048-seed digest; cold time is 87.0 seconds and warm time is 48.5 seconds, versus 62.7 seconds for the prior warm path—a 23% end-to-end improvement. The expanded focused producer/verifier/kernel matrix passes 45/45 and the complete repository suite passes 1,010/1,010.

V1 disposition: The initial full producer was intentionally interrupted after 12 create-only receipts and 953.9 seconds when observed throughput projected to several days. Its tree remains retained as partial evidence and will not be resumed or relabeled. V2 uses a fresh output and preregistration lineage; no v1 receipt is reused.

L-199 — All-query combined retrieval made restart- and reboot-safe

V2 flaw and disposition: Review found that v2 preregistration bound the volatile tmpfs READY digest and physical file identities. A process restart on the same host was safe, but a host reboot removes /dev/shm and necessarily creates new paths/inodes, so valid completed receipts could not be resumed under their frozen contract. V2 was intentionally stopped after four sealed cases and 345.1 seconds. Those receipts remain retained as partial evidence and are neither relabeled nor reused by v3.

V3 recovery contract: Preregistration now binds durable packed and DTW semantic content. Every launch either reuses an exactly unchanged, previously fully validated physical lease; fully validates an untrusted existing mirror; completes validation after a generation was atomically copied but READY was not published; or reconstructs the missing tmpfs mirror from the verified durable generation. Correctly named abandoned copy staging is reclaimed before capacity checks, while linked, malformed or unrecognized entries fail closed. Each launch has a create-only attempt receipt, and every case records its originating attempt lease. Resumption validates the complete case seal, semantic content and attempt lineage but permits physical identities to differ across attempts. A nonblocking OS producer lock refuses concurrent launches and is automatically released by process death or reboot.

Attempt-local safety: Packed and DTW physical identities are checked after every newly computed query. Any in-attempt path, device, inode, size, timestamp or mode drift refuses case publication. Semantic case digests deliberately exclude attempt-local physical identities, so the same exact retrieval remains semantically stable across a reboot. Outcome, label and final-period data remain unopened.

Correctness and measured performance: An end-to-end synthetic interruption test proves that attempt 2 can use a different resident lease, preserve attempt 1's sealed case byte-for-byte, compute only the missing case and publish the aggregate. Real-data full validation of the 9.2-GB generation passed content, record, lease and DTW identity gates in 204.0 seconds (164.7 resident, 3.2 packed records, 36.2 DTW). The common unchanged-lease restart path completed its generation preflight in 0.097 seconds. All 40 focused tests and all 1,018 repository tests pass; git diff --check is clean. Retrieval math, pruning, top-20 ordering and numeric tolerance are unchanged.

L-200 — Distinct-symbol seed concentration diagnosed and closed

V3 failure and disposition: The restart-safe producer correctly retained 329 sealed receipts, then stopped on query 1223e104…d8708d (APDN at 2013-02-28) because the fixed 2,048-row rigid-bound seed contained only six symbols and could not establish a distinct-symbol top-20 threshold. This was not benchmark drift, data corruption, memory exhaustion or a restart failure: 1,817,049 rows were eligible, but low-priced and zero-volume histories concentrated the lowest rigid bounds in a few symbols. The v3 partial tree remains immutable evidence and is not reused or relabeled.

V4 correction: An explicitly selected v2 search contract begins with the same stable 2,048-row prefix and doubles that prefix only while it lacks 20 distinct symbols, capped by the complete eligible universe. Prefix membership retains deterministic (bound, episode ID) ordering, overflow rows participate in the same stable bounded merge, and exact completion still admits equality boundaries and prunes only strict greater-than bounds. If the complete eligible universe genuinely has fewer than 20 symbols, the search now terminates with a precise refusal. Legacy callers retain the byte-identical v1 fixed-seed contract digest 989bb426…e7224.

Real-data proof: APDN expanded once to 4,096 rows (29 distinct seed symbols), closed with 20 distinct matches, evaluated 5,156 exact candidates and reported zero lower-bound excess. Its ordered episode IDs and hexadecimal distances exactly equal an independent fixed-4,096 differential run. A frozen OHAI authority query required no expansion and reproduced all 20 authority episode IDs, symbols, cutoffs, hexadecimal distances and tiers exactly, also with zero bound excess. The adaptive contract digest is 14957dac…3ec.

Verification boundary: Adversarial tests cover a seed dominated by one symbol, geometric stable-prefix expansion, complete-universe symbol exhaustion, legacy contract immutability and exhaustive-oracle parity. Producer and independent verifier tests bind the fresh v4 schemas and adaptive contract. All 26 focused tests and all 1,020 repository tests pass; Python compilation, HTML parsing and git diff --check are clean. The volatile packed mirror was also rebuilt from durable bytes and fully validated in 193.5 seconds. V4 writes to a new output tree, so no v1, v2 or v3 receipt can cross the lineage boundary.

L-201 — Full-batch harness audited, hardened and moved to a qualified host

V4 refusal and disposition: V4 published no query case and sealed an interruption after detecting process swap during startup on the former approximately 32-GiB host. This was a valid safety refusal, not retrieval evidence. Its contract, attempt and interruption receipts remain unchanged; v5 uses a fresh output lineage and cannot reuse or relabel them.

Independent audit findings: Review of the complete producer, verifier and adaptive search flow found no incorrect pruning or stable main/overflow merge result, including randomized oracle comparisons. It did find operational gaps: preregistration enumerated only selected runtime modules, resource suitability was checked too late, the RESULT-before-COMPLETE crash window was not reconciled, terminal receipts were under-validated, query input digests were trusted rather than independently rebuilt, and an empty attempt directory left by a precisely timed crash could inflate the terminal attempt count.

V5 corrections: Preregistration now binds every Python module in src/market_analogues, all experiment/config drivers and exact Python/package versions. Linux CPU, total/available memory, RSS/peak RSS and process swap are observed before resident setup, after resident validation, after preload and around every new query; the frozen minimum is eight effective CPUs, 64 GiB total, 32 GiB available and zero process swap. Case receipts preserve the full observation. Started and terminal attempts are structurally and cryptographically revalidated; empty pre-start crash directories remain safely ignored. A sealed RESULT is fully reconstructed from all case bytes and either checked against COMPLETE or used to atomically repair the missing COMPLETE/PROGRESS publications. Directory fsync closes the atomic progress durability boundary. A host-resize compatibility defect found by the broad suite was also corrected independently in the older exact-scheduler producer and verifier: when a systemd leaf cgroup exposes only usage counters, both now walk to the nearest ancestor whose cpu.stat includes the required quota/throttling counters, while retaining the leaf path and fail-closed qualification.

Independent verification: The verifier now rebuilds every query episode, causal stock-prefix digest, representation digest and search request, then independently reconstructs all 3,936 certificate input digests from fully validated packed/DTW manifests. It also requires the terminal COMPLETE receipt to bind the exact RESULT digest, rechecks adaptive doubling-ladder seed sizes and full resource records, and caches immutable OHLCV frames to avoid repeated Parquet reads.

Pre-launch evidence: The forced stable main/overflow tie case expands from a two-row single-symbol prefix through the complete six-row universe and finds all three symbols; all focused producer/search/verifier tests pass 35/35. All 1,029 repository tests pass after the live tmpfs mirror was rebuilt from durable bytes and fully validated in 170.7 seconds; compilation, HTML parsing and git diff --check pass. The host now exposes 94.3 GiB RAM, 12 logical CPUs and a 48-GiB tmpfs. The 11,513,989,110-byte NASDAQ walk_forward/merged_all.parquet was confirmed functionally reproducible from 12,080 retained validated OHLCV files, the retained IXIC benchmark and tracked feature/label/walk-forward scripts, then permanently removed; pip/npm caches were purged, increasing root free space from about 6.6 GiB to 28 GiB. Source OHLCV, benchmark, labels, models and prior evidence were not removed.

L-205 — True-composite topology POC prepared before scale

Why: Review confirmed that v5's historical “combined” name means the price component's rigid-plus-DTW completion, not the seven-group professional-chart composite. The true composite authority is already certified on three frozen probes, and an older 24-case process gate showed a 4.06× improvement, but neither result chooses a topology for the current 12-core host and 3,936-query monthly registry.

POC design: Four scored queries from the same frozen month run twice with equal 12-thread budgets: one fresh process with a single shared four-query packed scan and 12-thread exact completion, then four fresh one-query processes with three threads each. Both use the real seven-group distance weights, branch-aware safe bounds, requested-position vector completion, compact exact state, native-bound deferral and streaming closure. Every match, component, alignment, proposal digest and timing-free certificate must agree; OHAI must also reproduce the earlier frozen multichannel authority.

Failure/restart behavior: Preregistration binds all package runtime files, inputs, contracts, topology order and resource controls before measurement. Sequential and parallel receipts publish independently and are seal/semantic/certificate validated before reuse, so interruption reruns only the missing topology. Each worker requires an unchanged resident identity and zero process swap. The result selects speed only after all correctness gates pass; no outcome, label or final-period data is opened.

Checkpoint state: The new topology comparison and contract tests pass 2/2; the directly relevant packed-bound/certified-search matrix passes 32/32; all 1,033 repository tests pass. Python compilation, HTML parsing and git diff --check pass. The implementation is ready for its commit, sole-child preregistration and execution.

L-206 — Four-way true-composite topology wins at exact parity

Measured result: Sole-child preregistration d745beb froze the test before measurement. One process with 12 threads completed four same-month queries in 398.40 seconds; four independent three-thread groups completed in 171.04 seconds, a 2.329× speedup. Parallel worker wall times were 80.79, 112.95, 142.88 and 170.11 seconds. Aggregate worker peak RSS was 2,286.79 MiB (maximum one worker 596.08 MiB), versus 682.37 MiB sequential, with zero swap.

Correctness: Sequential and parallel outputs have identical proposal digests, seven component distances, alignments, timing-free certificates and ordered matches for all four queries. Each certificate accounts for its complete 1,961,577/1,961,578-row eligible universe with zero lower-bound excess. OHAI reproduces frozen multichannel certificate b4c4e167…2fad and all prior matches exactly. Producer result digest 19a98489…ab33.

Independent gate: A separate verifier is prepared to reconstruct all eight certificate digests, validate every seal and aggregate binding, resolve all 80 links against the fully rehashed packed generation, recheck temporal eligibility/distinct symbols/seven-component presence, and confirm the speed comparison and no-outcome boundary. Its focused test includes certificate-digest tampering. The independent verifier must be committed and pass before p4×3 is used by the all-query producer.

L-207 — First topology-verifier attempt refused physical layout mismatch

Failure: The committed independent verifier fully rehashed and record-validated the packed generation, then raised DTypePromotionError before link validation or receipt publication. It attempted to concatenate feature-bearing main rows with metadata-only overflow rows even though those immutable physical layouts intentionally have different field sets. The producer result and both topology receipts were not changed.

Correction: The verifier now projects both arrays into a new independent metadata-only dtype containing episode ID, cutoff, symbol ID and quality tier before concatenation. A regression test uses deliberately incompatible main/overflow extra fields and requires their three metadata rows to normalize exactly. Certificate, causality and result checks are unchanged. A new verifier commit and clean rerun are required; the failed invocation remains recorded here and cannot be called a pass.

L-208 — Second topology-verifier attempt exposed an unreachable return

Failure: The metadata projection patch was inserted between construction and return of the independent case-semantic payload. The helper therefore returned None, and the verifier refused every otherwise-valid case on semantic-digest mismatch. All eight independent certificate digest, accounting, component and diversity predicates were separately inspected and pass. No receipt was published.

Test weakness and repair: The first synthetic certificate test derived its expected digest from that same helper, so hashing None allowed the fixture to pass. The helper's return is restored before the metadata function, and the test now explicitly requires a dictionary exactly equal to the separately implemented producer semantics before computing any digest. This turns the precise structural defect into a permanent regression failure.

L-209 — Independent true-composite topology verification passes

Result: Verifier commit a5cd078 passes every frozen gate in 30.61 seconds. It independently reconstructed all eight certificate digests, validated all seals/aggregate bindings, fully rehashed and record-validated the packed generation, resolved all 80 analogue IDs and rechecked symbols, quality, cutoffs, query overlap, distinctness and seven-component presence. Observation digest c38e86ac…1d23; verification digest 9ef7ecaf…0584.

Interim decision: Four process groups with three threads each beat a single 12-thread group. This is a measured host-specific execution result, not a change to distance, eligibility or ranking. L-210 explicitly tests the still-wider alternative before the all-query topology is frozen.

L-210 — Wider 12-core topology check required before full freeze

Review correction: The verified four-query POC proves p4×3 beats p1×12, but four queries cannot exercise p12×1. Therefore L-209's p4 selection is provisional rather than the final fastest-host claim. Older eight-process evidence and ample 94-GiB memory make the wider topology credible enough that assuming against it would violate the measured-optimization rule.

Final width POC: Select the first 12 episode IDs in ascending order from the 24 scored March-2014 registry rows—before timing—and compare p4×3 with p12×1 under equal 12-core budgets. Every timing-free proposal, certificate, component, alignment and match must agree; all 24 certificates must close and swap must remain zero. Topology receipts are independent and resumable. No outcome or label is opened.

L-216 — One-shot completion listener replaces polling

Mechanism: A separate Git-bound listener opens Linux pidfd for the exact producer process and blocks once in the kernel with no timed status loop. L-217 — V1 strict-JSON interruption diagnosed; V2 preserves the proof

Observed failure: V1 attempt 0001 stopped after 57 create-only cases at 2026-09-07 10:31:56 UTC. Python's strict JSON encoder rejected a non-finite float before the failing worker could return; the listener correctly published producer_incomplete and did not launch verification. No sealed case was corrupted or overwritten.

Exact diagnosis: A 12-process rerun of the entire in-flight window found one and only one non-finite value: CRDS query af773e…18bc, certificate path rounds[0].constrained_threshold. Its first 512 exact candidates supplied only 17 distinct symbols, so the constrained selector correctly used positive infinity until the frontier expanded. All 240 final matches, component distances, alignments and worker measurements were finite; the other 11 queries contained no non-finite value. Diagnostic digest 3a6cb6a0…0597.

V2 correction: Contract-valid intermediate positive infinity is now encoded as the explicit strict-JSON object {"__nonfinite_float__":"positive_infinity"}. It is allowed only for an uncertified round with fewer than 20 selected symbols, or a certified exhaustive closure upper bound. NaN, negative infinity, final thresholds, match values and every unexpected path still fail closed. Independent verification decodes the sentinel solely to reconstruct the original certificate digest.

Real regression: The exact CRDS worker now completes six rounds, records 17 selected rows and the explicit sentinel in round zero, returns 20 finite matches with zero swap, and passes independent certificate reconstruction at unchanged search-result digest 15e97ea0…e9b02. All 1,061 repository tests pass. V1 remains immutable evidence. V2 uses a new output root and preregistration; the 57 V1 receipts are intentionally not relabelled under a different contract, costing approximately one early wave rather than weakening provenance.

L-221 — Pre-outcome audit finds walk-forward symbol-exclusion mismatch

Finding: Before constructing predictions, the four retrieval stores were reconciled against the frozen walk-forward contract rather than only their own generic-search contracts. The generic rule permits old non-overlapping episodes from the query stock, while the walk-forward contract requires same_query_symbol_excluded=true. The internally valid stores contain 78 composite links in 78 queries, 94 price links in 94 queries and 40 baseline links in 40 queries (16 deterministic-random and 24 return/volatility). Therefore L-220 remains valid evidence for generic exact retrieval, but WF-03 is not yet contract-complete and none of these inventories may feed predictions unchanged.

Scope reconciliation: The four methods contain 314,880 links over 274,343 unique episodes. The verified T14-09 outcome store covers 56,378 episodes from the separate 3,270-query present snapshot and overlaps the walk-forward union on only 7,954 episodes; as many as 266,389 additional unique episode outcomes may be required before per-link causal availability is applied.

Repair: Preserve all old receipts. An unaffected certified top-20 is already exact over the stricter subset when it contains no query-symbol row. For each affected query, certify top-21 under the original one-per-symbol superset and remove its sole query-symbol row, which yields the exact top-20 under the frozen exclusion rule. Baselines receive the equivalent independent-order reconstruction. A new cross-store manifest and verifier must prove 3,936 × four complete, distinct, non-query-symbol inventories before analogue outcomes are expanded. Historical query outcomes and the final period remain unopened.

It rejects duplicate symbols, malformed rows, invalid k and an affected prefix without the necessary extra row.

Audit harness: A create-only producer binds the frozen walk-forward contract, registry, three producer results and their independent verification receipts. It reconstructs all four method inventories from 3,936 sealed query cases, validates query identity and no-outcome flags, rejects conflicting episode identities and publishes exact affected-query IDs plus T14-09 outcome overlap. L-232 — Full repair v1 stopped safely; top-21 serializer defect diagnosed

Observed: V1 completed and sealed all 40 baseline repairs, all 94 price-only repairs and one composite repair (135/212). A subsequent composite search completed correctly but publication stopped with CompositeTopologyError: invalid infinite round threshold. No partial or invalid receipt was written; the terminal result and manifest do not exist.

Root cause: The inherited strict-JSON certificate encoder treated 20 selected rows as a complete top-k because its validation boundary was hard-coded to the historical top-20 search. For the new top-21 contract, an intermediate round with 20 distinct rows correctly retains a positive-infinity constrained threshold. Retrieval, pruning, distance calculation and selection were not implicated.

Repair: The encoder now accepts an explicit positive required_top_k; historical callers retain the top-20 default, while both exclusion-repair producers pass 21. A boundary test proves that selected=20/+∞ is accepted for top-21 and still rejected for top-20. V1 remains immutable failure evidence.

V2 reuse rule: A new create-only V2 contract will bind the V1 contract, exact interrupted progress, and an ordered digest of all 135 sealed receipts. V2 may copy each only after full seal, query, source-file, old-prefix and subset-proof validation; imported receipts are resealed under V2 with their V1 digest and SHA-256. Only the remaining 77 composite receipts will be recomputed.

Pre-freeze validation: The legacy-evidence audit reconstructs the exact 1/94/16/24 receipt split and digest 9d88e760…b6d2. A temporary create-only migration validated, imported and resealed all 135 receipts. The focused repair/migration suite passes 27/27; Python compilation, strict diff checks and all 1,092 repository tests pass. L-234 — D2 cross-store manifest implementation and real preflight

Representation: D2 writes one compact Parquet row per query/method/rank position. Each row retains query and fold identity, canonical packed episode identity, exact hexadecimal distance, effective-list and match digests, resolution kind, latest eligible cutoff and the path, SHA-256 and seal digest of its source case or repair receipt. Large composite alignments are not duplicated; their sealed source artifact remains losslessly addressable through that binding.

Episode requests: A second table deduplicates the effective link union by episode ID and retains dataset, symbol, cutoff and quality tier. Packed main and overflow rows are searched together; symbol, cutoff and tier must agree with each source match, and every matched cutoff must be no later than the query's sealed latest-eligible timestamp.

Lifecycle: A clean-H0/sole-child-H1 preregistration freezes the D1 result, manifest and verification bytes/digests, registry, resident packed generation, exact logical table digests and counts. Publication uses a temporary sibling directory followed by one atomic rename, so interruption cannot expose a partial official store. The independent verifier reconstructs every row from D1 sources and the packed inventory and checks exact directory/file closure.

Evidence so far: The real outcome-blind preflight reconstructed all 314,880 links and found 274,331 unique episode requests in 27.89 seconds. Link digest was 6ea31e6e…16c9 before the final registry-order correction; the frozen digest will be regenerated from the corrected authoritative order. Seventeen focused D1/D2 tests pass, including synthetic refusals for duplicate ranks, query-symbol leakage, future leakage, missing/conflicting requests, ordering mutation and null mutation.

Boundary and next: No outcome was opened. Finish the complete repository suite, commit H0, generate and commit only the preregistration as H1, publish D2, then run its independent verifier from a clean commit. D3 remains unauthorized until that receipt passes.

L-236 — D3 causal outcome-store implementation and bounded real proof

Frozen work shape: D2 contributes 274,331 unique episode requests. Exact ID/cutoff/source-fingerprint/contract/source-content reconciliation permits reuse of 7,955 episodes from the independently verified T14-09 store; the other 266,376 require new computation. The missing inventory digest is b5b89e6a…eb00.

Memory/performance design: Concurrency and restart granularity are separated. Forty-eight deterministic symbol partitions contain 4,700–7,116 episodes each and run through 12 processes, retaining full CPU use while reducing the simultaneous in-memory future-path working set by approximately fourfold versus twelve oversized partitions. A symbol is loaded in only one partition and each completed partition is atomically sealed for restart.

Outputs: The final atomic store contains six-horizon outcome rows, all observed normalized future-path rows, explicit censor/ambiguity status and a 1,889,280-row query/method/rank/horizon eligibility table. Eligibility is true only for complete outcomes whose completion timestamp is no later than the query cutoff; incomplete and not-yet-observable reasons remain distinct.

Verification: The independent verifier uses the separately implemented T14-09 reference oracle to recompute every new episode and compare every outcome and path row. Reused rows must equal the prior independently verified store. It separately reconstructs causal eligibility, coverage, partition aggregation and physical/semantic file closure. Operational incident: The one-shot shell listener watched the noncanonical shorthand t14-10-wf03d-outcomes-v1 while the committed producer correctly published to t14-10-wf03d-outcome-store-v1. This caused a missed automatic handoff only: no data failed, no partition was rerun and no artifact was changed. The verifier was started directly in a persistent execution session and completed normally. Future handoffs must derive the output path from the producer constant rather than duplicate it.

Next: Implement D4 without opening historical query futures: freeze the prediction formulas and all abstention/novelty fields, synthesize causal month-by-month fixtures, obtain an independent formula oracle, then commit H0 and a sole-child preregistration before producing any real prediction.

L-238 — D4 prediction-before-outcome implementation and real path preflight

Access-control finding: Thirty-five of the 3,936 registry episode IDs also occur in D3 because those historical states are legitimate causally completed analogues for later queries. None belongs to the 480-query final block. D4 therefore treats analogue evidence and query-label state as separate namespaces: expanding unconditional/regime baselines may consume only prior query outcomes published behind a month receipt after that month’s predictions sealed.

Prediction surface: Each query receives composite rank-weighted and unweighted diagnostics plus price-only, deterministic-random and return/volatility evidence at prefixes 5/10/15/20. Outputs include half-smoothed three-class barrier-route and directional probabilities, exclusion counts, effective sample size, novelty, neighborhood instability, forced/selective lanes, five weighted quantiles for four measures at six horizons and 126-step pointwise path medians for five path measures. Unconditional and causal benchmark-regime frequencies use the same smoothing/censor rules.

Temporal lifecycle: Each of 164 month directories first atomically publishes four prediction files and PREDICTIONS_SEALED.json. Only then may a non-final month compute and publish query outcomes and MONTH_CLOSED.json. January 2024 through August 2025 permit prediction files only. Partial non-final outcome publication resumes only when recomputed bytes match; unexpected files, changed inputs or final-period outcomes fail closed.

Performance/correctness POC: The real D3 path table has 33,383,810 rows across 274,153 episode IDs; 178 episodes correctly have no observable path row. The first implementation incorrectly assumed every episode had a path and was repaired before freeze. A one-time path index now builds in approximately 7.5 seconds. Preparing global joins once and vectorizing all 126 weighted medians reduced a real 24-query month from approximately 14 seconds to approximately 3.6 seconds after preparation while matching the scalar formula to floating-point accumulation roundoff.

Synthetic boundary: Focused prediction/scoring tests currently pass 38/38. They cover original-rank weights, half smoothing, directional/no-touch exclusion, ambiguous/censored/unobservable accounting, completion equality, future mutation invariance, missing paths, scalar/vector agreement, kernel-level process-exit listening and rejection of final-period outcome files. The standalone synthetic verifier must pass from clean H0 before D4 preregistration.

Complete-suite diagnostics: The first full run reached one expected index-sensitive failure because the new package module was not yet staged and therefore was absent from git ls-files; that gate passed immediately after staging the exact D4 set. The next run encountered an unrelated readiness-file identity race in an existing serial-runtime adversarial test; the exact case passed five consecutive isolated reruns. A third full run over the identical staged code completed at 100% with exit code zero. Neither diagnostic changed D4 calculations or opened query outcomes.

Next: Run the complete repository suite, freeze implementation H0, run the clean-commit synthetic verifier, preregister its exact receipt as sole-child H1, then execute/resume the 164-month producer and the independent all-row verifier. Evaluation metrics and final outcomes remain unopened.

L-239 — D4 v1 query-outcome scope refusal and v2 repair

Retained refusal: V1 H0 53ec33c and sole-child H1 9fac9d3 froze preregistration e92209f1…02a0. It sealed and closed January–September 2012 and sealed October predictions, then stopped before October query outcomes because the full source frame for registry stock MHR contains non-positive OHLC bars in March–May 2016. No producer seal exists; the pidfd listener correctly refused to launch verification. The ten prediction receipts and nine closed-month receipts remain immutable failed-prefix evidence and will not be reused.

Diagnosis: The MHR query cutoff is 31 October 2012; its frozen calculation requires the prior 20 ATR bars and at most 126 subsequent stock bars, ending 3 May 2013. The invalid 2016 rows cannot affect any value for that query. Rejecting the entire lifetime source was therefore an overly broad operational validation scope, not a bad query or permission to impute data.

V2 rule: Preserve the full-file fingerprint for identity, but pass exactly [cutoff−20 stored rows, cutoff+126 stored rows] to the unchanged outcome calculator. No bar inside that window is removed or repaired; any non-positive, non-finite, inconsistent or duplicate local observation still fails closed. The independent oracle applies the same causal extent through separately implemented slicing.

L-242 — Synthetic input-order refusal and deterministic aggregation repair

Retained refusal: The first post-H0 synthetic execution reached the final input-order transformation and refused before publishing a receipt. Reversing rows changed a synthetic mean from 0.012499999999999999 to 0.012499999999999997; no real query score, aggregate or final outcome was opened.

Cause and repair: Pandas reductions preserve physical row order, so equivalent inputs can accumulate floating-point additions differently. The producer now canonically sorts by query ID and lane before fold/stability/calibration aggregations, by query ID before risk ties, and by query ID before paired monthly inference. The independent oracle applies the same externally specified canonical order through separate code.

L-249 — WF-04 failure diagnosis independently verified

Integrity: Diagnostic store bf60595d…d93f0 contains 13,920 paired query/baseline rows, 1,145 all-cell segment rows, 126 probability-behaviour rows and 580 month/baseline rows. The independent verifier audited every source pairing and recomputed every formula; all six gates passed in 3.74 seconds at verification digest 02b60a89…43d5e.

Stable finding: Composite skill versus unconditional is −3.09% in development, −3.35% validation 1, +0.46% validation 2, −7.16% validation 3, −3.22% pooled validation and −3.46% final. It improves individual query Brier in 46/116 calendar months and 48.9% of pooled-validation queries; neither is stable evidence of conditional value.

Mechanism: In final, actual routes are 27.3% favorable, 67.3% adverse and 5.5% no-touch. Composite predicts 31.0%, 60.1% and 8.9%, respectively, with one-v-rest AUCs 0.502, 0.522 and 0.558. It is sharper than unconditional but barely discriminates individual favorable/adverse cases. Pooled validation shows a real descriptive gain on rare no-touch rows (+11.16% skill) that is overwhelmed by −19.76% class-conditional skill on the much more common adverse rows; final favorable and adverse rows both lose.

Post-hoc warning: Some cells look positive in one period but reverse elsewhere—for example, pooled low-liquidity is near flat while final high-liquidity loses 9.06%; final down/high-volatility gains descriptively while up/high-volatility loses. None may be selected as a validated V2 rule.

L-252 — Stockbee risk-set V1 source-quality refusal and V2 repair

L-256 — Stockbee clustered-event prevalence independently verified

Frozen boundary: H0 d4b90d4, sole-child H1 6f2d362 and preregistration 8164bafa…d2de fixed all populations, horizons, exposures and windows before prevalence was opened.

Execution: Producer 7066fea2…0ab4 clustered 4,034,853 overlapping qualifying windows into 528,114 distinct event runs (248,113 at 21 sessions; 280,001 at 63) and published 192 complete prevalence cells in 32.49 seconds. The independent implementation reread all 37,358,636 risk rows and exactly reconstructed every cluster, event row, denominator and numerator. All six gates passed in 32.80 seconds at verification ba35db25…00d5b.

What is supported descriptively: A +4% close day occurs somewhere in 99.78% of broad 21-session events and 98.70% of broad 63-session events. Within the first five sessions it occurs in 70.28% and 52.99%; investable rates are 66.77% and 44.73%. Bullish range expansion within five sessions occurs in 51.25%/43.28% broad and 50.57%/40.26% investable events.

What is not supported literally: Exact-start +4% close moves occur in only 25.95% of broad 21-session and 17.81% of broad 63-session events; bullish exact-start expansions occur in 16.12% and 13.27%. Therefore “contains one or a series” is strongly descriptive, while “majority started on the exact first day” is not supported by this frozen operational definition.

Why controls still matter: In the investable unclustered risk set, first-five +4% close prevalence is 71.13% among 21-session winners versus 25.66% among non-winners, and 47.96% versus 24.93% at 63 sessions. These gaps are promising but overlapping and unmatched; they are not the final controlled estimate.

Next: Construct deterministic same-date matched controls, retain relaxation/shortfall accounting, calculate paired calendar-month block inference and apply the preregistered Holm family before deciding claim support.

L-257 — Stockbee matched-control and inference stage ready

Frozen interpretation: Four causal matching variables receive average-rank deciles separately inside each population/date/horizon cross-section. Investable events therefore match against the investable cross-section. Five controls are unique within an event; a non-winner may be reused for a different event because the contract's SHA-256 order is event-specific. The first relaxation tier containing five candidates is used, while final-tier shortfalls remain explicit.

Primary estimands: Matched inference admits only events with all five controls and reports complete-event coverage. Full-risk inference compares clustered winner-event prevalence with unclustered non-winner prevalence within calendar month. Matched inference uses the monthly mean of event exposure minus the mean exposure of its five controls. Both receive a three-month moving-block, 10,000-resample one-sided positive-effect test and percentile interval; all 32 frozen primary cells share one Holm family.

Implementation and verifier: The producer performs one column-pruned load of the 37,358,636-row verified risk store, streams control and event-match Parquet rather than retaining millions of Python records, and publishes atomically. The verifier uses a separate rank, bucket, SHA selection, bootstrap and Holm implementation and checks every control identity, rank, decile, exposure, aggregate and decision.

Pre-real gates: Seventeen focused Stockbee tests pass, including tied deciles, exact/relaxed/shortfall matching, unique deterministic hash order and an independently coded bootstrap oracle. Review also corrected a prospective full-risk denominator scope before preregistration; no matched-control result has been opened.

Next: Run the complete repository suite, commit this implementation/log as H0, generate and commit only its preregistration JSON as sole-child H1, then execute the real producer and committed independent verifier.

L-259 — Verifier V2 preregistered but rejected at preflight; V3 cache-key repair

V2 boundary: H0 94fba52 and sole-child preregistration cf162de were frozen, but no V2 execution or receipt was produced. V2's proposed cache key included that transient identity and therefore would still have rebuilt buckets per event.

L-260 — Stockbee controlled study independently verified

Certification: V3 H0 8e1d447, sole-child H1 754067e and preregistration 20426401…da6e bind the optimized verifier to unchanged producer digest 55da5a46…25298. In 1,107.66 seconds it independently reconstructed all 3,551,826 controls, 710,373 event/population match rows, every decile and exposure value, 32 monthly bootstrap/Holm tests and the claim decision. All six gates passed at verification digest f6594c37…c7671.

Coverage: 710,364/710,373 event/population rows have all five controls (99.9987%). Only nine events have shortfalls; every shortfall remains in the audited event-match table. The broad 21/63 coverage rates are 99.9980%/99.9996%; investable rates are 99.9961%/100%.

Controlled findings: Broad 21-session events show first-five +4% close prevalence of 70.28% versus 51.30% in matched controls (RR 1.37); investable events show 66.77% versus 42.79% (RR 1.56). First-five bullish range expansion is 51.25% versus 37.41% broad (RR 1.37) and 50.57% versus 34.25% investable (RR 1.48). Broad 63-session +4% close prevalence is 52.99% versus 42.34% (RR 1.25), but investable prevalence is only 44.73% versus 33.01% (RR 1.35).

Decision: Ten of 32 primary cells meet every frozen requirement; all adjusted one-sided p-values are 0.0032 and all effects point the same way across broad/investable populations. The first-five-session majority statement is supported for one-month events, and for broad—but not investable—quarter events. No exact-start cell reaches 50%, so the literal claim that a majority begin on the first day with a 4% expansion is not supported.

Scientific boundary: These tests intentionally replicate a retrospective statement. The 4% exposure occurs inside the same 21/63-session window used to define the 25% outcome, so part of the association is mechanically embedded in that outcome. It is not evidence that buying after the 4% day has positive expectancy. The next study must move time zero to the close of the observed signal and measure only subsequent returns against causal same-date controls and market regime.

Next: Freeze T14-12 predictive inversion before calculating it: signal at the first qualifying +4% close or bullish range-expansion day, outcomes beginning next session, benchmark-relative 5/20/60-session returns and barriers, causal matching including NASDAQ regime, and negative-control/base-rate comparisons. Only a verified post-signal advantage may become an analogue retrieval feature.

Matching variables end at t−1, while exact-date matching fixes shared market regime.

L-268 — T14-12 continuous-rank V2 matcher ready for preflight

Design: Convert all four frozen t−1 covariates to average percentile ranks inside each same-date population. For each signal-specific equal-risk non-signal pool, select the five smallest four-dimensional squared rank distances; resolve exact distance ties by the already frozen contract/signal/candidate SHA-256 order.

Performance and exactness: A cKDTree obtains a 16-candidate frontier for all events in a date batch. If a tie reaches the frontier, a radius query expands it; every admitted frontier is then exactly rescored and sorted. Randomized, tied and shortfall tests reproduce an exhaustive all-candidate oracle exactly.

Verification: The independent implementation rebuilds every percentile rank, spatial frontier, exact distance, identity and output row. Three deterministic dates per year also compare the optimized frontier against exhaustive selection. The V2 namespace is separate, so the rejected V1 artifacts remain reproducible.

All 12 current/historical × A/B × low/middle/high cases pass, with 240 exact matches, 720 5/20/60-session evidence rows, zero selected-match rescore delta, identical repeated evidence digests and an unchanged source-universe prefix.

Coverage is explicit: 239, 236 and 231 of the 240 retrieved episodes have complete outcomes observable by the corresponding query cutoff at 5, 20 and 60 sessions. Missing, truncated or not-yet-observable paths remain present and labelled rather than being replaced. All 13 generated HTML files parse and retain the descriptive-not-forecast boundary.

Bindings: Retrieval verification e85a2c17…7567dc; NSE evidence result 982cf6ca…7d42d; semantic universe prefix cffc06d9…d787; maximum prefix cutoff 11 February 2026. The gate ledger records E2E-03 PASS with no failures.

Next: E2E-04 will bind the already independently sealed T14-05 restart/concurrency/resource qualification to this release path and add current-artifact corruption, atomicity and resume checks. It will not rerun expensive evidence merely to reproduce an already sealed performance measurement.

Pre-E2E-04 receipt audit: The successful V4 computation remains valid, but its writer omitted registry_digest from the persisted dataclass even though that value participated in the semantic result digest. A third party therefore could not reconstruct the digest from the saved file alone. V5 adds that field without changing retrieval or outcome logic; E2E-04 will bind only the reconstructible V5 receipt.

Final release binding: V5 passed with identical retrieval/outcome counts and reconstructible digest 2c4c6b11…2eadba. E2E-04 and the release index bind V5, not the earlier V4 publication.

L-282 — E2E-04 operational certificate implemented before execution

Reuse decision: T14-05 already has an independently verified real SIGKILL/failed-closed test, clean-root restart, P1/P2/P4/P8 semantic parity, three full repeated runs and a 5,221-sample process-tree resource monitor with zero swap and zero OOM events. E2E-04 re-runs that independent reconstruction and binds its immutable digests rather than spending hours repeating exposed evidence.

Current-path additions: The certificate reconstructs the V5 NSE receipt, authenticates the fresh authority verifier, checks all 12 strict-stop/repeated/resume certificates, mutates a copied real authority and requires rejection, scans all current trees for partial files, parses all 13 NSE reports and proves the physical benchmark now extends beyond the still-stable semantic prefix.

Resource boundary: The fresh 12-worker run persisted elapsed time and disk footprint but not peak RSS. The certificate reports that absence explicitly and applies the independently sealed core resource observation; it does not manufacture a fresh-run memory number from transient monitoring.

V1 retained refusal: All current-path and operational gates passed, but the aggregate included the cross-adapter receipt's publication-only generated_at field when reconstructing its semantic digest. That timestamp was intentionally absent from the original digest. V1 therefore failed only portable_cross_adapter_bound. V2 excludes exactly that publication field, adds a boundary test and re-runs every gate; V1 remains immutable.

L-285 — E2E-05 release snapshot passes

L-286 — Post-release literature/repository refresh freezes the next POCs

Method: After E2E closure, two parallel gap audits compared the current implementation and earlier research against primary papers and active repositories. The installed Deep Research workflow was followed; its executor was not callable in this environment, so primary-source web research and direct repository evidence supplied the cited HTML artifact.

Finding: No inspected repository is a drop-in replacement for the causal, exact-certified multichannel system. The material gaps are neighborhood adequacy/hubness, fixed-window maturity, a single shared channel-warp path, forced static neighbors instead of evolving chains, and outcome summaries that hide distinct continuation/failure modes.

Frozen order: R1 exposure-controlled adequacy certificate; R2 deterministic outcome-path modes with neighbor identity fixed; R3 variable-duration/channel-lag distance-v2 POC; R4 synthetic feature-envelope expansion; then optional soft-alignment, complexity/topology, wavelet, path-signature and local-fragment explanations. Learned embeddings remain deferred. Every new metric is outcome-blind and must earn its own authority before promotion.

Preliminary diagnostic: The sealed 65,400-match NASDAQ store has 56,378 distinct matched episodes; 90.13% occur once, the maximum is 38 and the top 1% account for 5.57%. This does not prove hubness because eligibility exposure differs, so no ranking change is authorized before R1 normalizes by causal opportunity.

L-287 — R1-A exact exposure audit implemented and corrected before inference

What and why: R1-A asks whether the same historical episodes or symbols recur more often than their causal opportunity alone would imply. It reads the sealed NASDAQ retrieval graph but excludes all forward outcomes, so it can diagnose neighborhood structure without tuning similarity toward returns.

Production-rule correction: the first design incorrectly assumed one selected episode per symbol. A source-level audit established the actual selector contract: A/B-tier eligibility, ordering by total distance and episode ID, at most three episodes per symbol, inclusive rejection of overlapping same-symbol windows, then top-20 stopping. No result was accepted under the wrong rule. The null was changed to a uniform random episode ordering over each exact causal risk set followed by that unchanged greedy selector, and a direct differential test now compares the null's acceptance behavior with production _select_compact_scored.

Hardening: the audit reconstructs the upstream case manifest, registry and seal digests; validates packed rows, byte hashes, provenance and every symbol's stride-five/lookback-252 row accounting; includes both main and overflow tiers; binds all runtime/input hashes in preregistration; uses metric-specific concentration tails and full declared populations for top-one-percent shares; refuses publication if any gate fails; and handles an exact zero rank-one distance explicitly. Seven focused tests cover permutation exhaustiveness, production equivalence, statistics and edge conditions.

Retained failure: preregistered v1 completed its simulation but a post-processing NameError occurred before any result directory was published. V1 remains frozen. The corrected v2 preregistration explicitly records the earlier one-replicate engineering smoke and v1 execution exposure, so it makes no pristine inferential-holdout claim.

L-288 — R1-A full causal concentration result deterministically replayed

Result: v2 completed 512 preregistered null replicates across all 3,270 queries, 65,400 selected links, 3,786,156 candidate episodes, 11,584 symbols and 106 eligibility cutoffs in 122.23 seconds using 12 workers. The producer passes all ten gates at digest b22ec5f4…a4e2a; the first verifier reconstructs all 512 replicates and passes all eleven gates at e31c31b6…a4df.

Measured structure: observed episode maximum reuse is 38 versus null median 3 and p99 4; episode unique count is 56,378 versus null median 64,840; symbol maximum is 90 versus null median 37 and p99 44; repeated-symbol query fraction is 13.76% versus null median 3.21% and p99 3.91%; mean pairwise query episode overlap is 0.004082 versus null median 0.000106. Every preregistered metric lies beyond all 512 null replicates in its concentration direction, giving the finite-sample Monte Carlo floor 1/513.

Correct interpretation: the distance-ranked graph contains far more shared structure than independent random priorities over identical causal risk sets. This is not yet evidence that the structure is bad: real recurring archetypes, common market regimes, correlated query states and metric domination can produce the same observation. The verifier is accurately described as a deterministic replay because it reuses producer helpers; the pure selector additionally has an exhaustive permutation oracle and a production-selector differential test.

Verifier correction: a release audit confirmed the stored numerical chain but found that the first verifier's result_digest_reconstructed gate checked RESULT self-consistency without separately checking the preregistration digest/runtime bindings or reconstructing every derived RESULT section. The preregistered producer, verifier and tests were not edited because their bytes are frozen. A new create-only post-publication integrity verifier instead validates all preregistered input/runtime hashes, binds RESULT to the preregistration and first receipt, replays all nulls, and reconstructs every comparison, inventory, geometry and exposure table. All 22 gates pass at semantic digest 36d80c00…f3c1. The tracked auditor byte SHA-256 is b12d7dd8…df93; the Git-ignored local receipt byte SHA-256 is 9cfbf5f6…0633.

Provenance note: the RESULT and first VERIFIED payloads use a …-v1 schema identifier while belonging to the corrected v2 execution namespace. This records an unchanged payload schema, not the failed v1 execution. The output paths, preregistration digest and result bindings disambiguate the lineages.

Repository verification: the complete suite passes 1,234/1,234 tests in 309.83 seconds. All 38 tracked HTML documents parse, local links from the three updated human documents resolve, git diff --check is clean, and git fsck --full reports no object corruption (unreferenced dangling blobs are retained Git objects, not integrity failures).

R1-B design correction: source and graph inspection found that literal causal reciprocity cannot exist for distinct episodes: if older A is legal for later B, later B is necessarily future information for A. The causal graph is a DAG, and all 3,936 walk-forward cases correctly contain zero mutual directed edges. R1-B will use symmetric offline pair geometry only as a descriptive consistency check; it will not misreport the structurally forced zero as retrieval failure.

Decision and next gate: retrieval remains byte-identical. No adequacy label, predictive claim, ranking change or production promotion is authorized. R1-B must distinguish meaningful precedent from generic hubs using matched and shared-structure nulls, symmetric offline geometry, local contrast and leave-one-group/input/cutoff stability; R1-C may calibrate labels only on synthetic/development cases.

Reproduce: run the frozen producer and replay only into absent create-only output roots: PYTHONPATH=.:src .venv/bin/python experiments/m04r/m04r14_r1a_exposure_audit.py --repository /home/vinay/code/market-analogues --workers 12, then PYTHONPATH=.:src .venv/bin/python experiments/m04r/verify_m04r14_r1a_exposure_audit.py --repository /home/vinay/code/market-analogues --workers 12. Audit the published chain with PYTHONPATH=.:src .venv/bin/python experiments/m04r/audit_m04r14_r1a_exposure_result.py --repository /home/vinay/code/market-analogues --workers 12. Local report: R1-A v2.

L-289 — R1-B is split into reuse-only, bounded exact and conditional stages

Why: the stored exact top 20 can answer local-distance and production-set stability questions but cannot certify which outside candidates enter after a component or channel changes. Re-running every variant for all 3,270 current queries would be slow and scientifically premature.

Evidence audit: existing sealed artifacts provide 3,270 current exact queries, 3,936 historical queries over 164 month cutoffs, 1,152 development queries, 65 same-symbol query pairs within 40 calendar days, 122 within 65 days and complete causal-risk-set metadata. Packed and DTW stores are safe quantized bounds, not exact representations.

Selected sequence: the new R1-B HTML contract freezes a reuse-only B0 for local geometry, empirical peer references, historical cutoff stability and shared-priority sensitivity; a 24-query one-pass/multi-variant certified B1 for true set stability; then a conditional B2 test of all recurrent episodes with inbound count at least five. A support-only pilot freezes nuisance/structure cells before any B2 statistic is opened.

Safeguards: no causal reciprocity metric; no top-20-only claim of set stability; component and channel ablation remain distinct; nearby-cutoff common and evolving risk sets remain distinct; all final/locked folds stay outside development; outcome-related stores are forbidden. R1-B cannot authorize prediction, trading, ranking changes or trader-facing adequacy labels.

L-290 — R1-B support-only runner hardened before preregistration

What: the B0-02 support substep now has exact conditional-set support combinatorics, deterministic balanced volatility-rank cells, deterministic farthest-first structure cells with small-cell merging, explicit causal eligibility boundaries and packed/baseline alignment helpers. The focused command passes 18 tests across support, adequacy and R1-A integrity.

Pre-result corrections: review removed benchmark/relative channels from the primary structure partition, removed packed bitfields as numeric features, reconstructed context/history availability, froze session-bin relaxation as 21→42→63, required both base and structure support, persisted per-query cells/medoids and per-episode support, and made support-only claim fields literal. A/B eligibility and the same-symbol start boundary remain exact.

Performance correction: an engineering preflight was terminated during I/O before support values were printed or published. It revealed that the 8.6-GB packed generation was being fully verified twice. The runner now performs one content-verified load, derives compact metadata from that mapping and indexes only the 3,270 needed baseline rows.

Freeze protocol: this H0 commit must precede the create-only preregistration. The preregistration binds the seven input authorities, all runtime/test hashes, known R1-A cohort inventory, exact feature indices, support formula/denominators, cell order/ties, support gates and claim boundary. It always binds the durable packed manifest; a byte-identical resident copy may only accelerate execution. No-overwrite hard-link publication protects the preregistration, while fsynced result files and a flock-serialized no-replace rename protect the result directory. Only the sole-child H1 commit may execute the support pilot.

L-291 — R1-B support v1 failed closed; v2 reconstructs exact query features

Failure boundary: committed v1 passed its input/runtime checks and verified the packed generation once, then stopped before computing or publishing any support value. The invalid assumption was that each current query identity must be a physical row in the five-session-stride historical pack. Read-only diagnosis found only 9 of 3,270 query IDs present and 3,261 absent; the failed output root remains absent.

Correction: v2 uses the historical pack only for its intended causal-candidate metadata. It reconstructs each current query's exact, unquantized distance-v1 representation and recent-return volatility from that query's registered 252-session stock and benchmark prefixes. It verifies the episode ID and both semantic causal-prefix digests against the sealed registry before forming any cell. No nearest packed row or other proxy is permitted.

Pre-freeze evidence: all 3,270 real registered queries reconstructed as finite 141-value market-excluded vectors with 3,270 finite volatility values in 210.27 seconds at 587.49 MiB peak RSS; audit digest 25f4af19…c5e613. A 23-test focused command now covers exact vector/volatility equality, 1-vs-12-worker identity/order and refusal of mutated episode, stock-prefix and benchmark-prefix identities. The v2 preregistration generator will disclose and bind the exact v1 H0/H1/digest/failure history, the 9/3,261 inventory, data configuration and source lock before another sole-child H1 may open support statistics.

L-292 — R1-B v2 support is independently verified; one-cell N1 is blocked

Producer result: committed H0 25382e1a…a42d and sole-preregistration H1 5254b7d5…dfd8 produced the create-only v2 support result at semantic digest e1d774d2…8dd42a. Its 3,270 exact current-query representations have audit digest 25f4af19…c5e613. The four published files have SHA-256 values 17d982ea…6218 (cohort support), 36e14f1e…0721cc (query cells), 51aa6cc4…71abd (RESULT) and fa2a07ff…bf54b (HTML).

Base support: the 21-session design supports 359/369 recurrent episodes (97.29%) and 2,808/2,865 inbound links (98.01%), above both frozen 90% gates. The 42-session design supports 362 episodes/2,829 links; the 63-session design supports all 369/2,865. The preregistered first-passing rule selects 21 sessions. Ten episodes and 57 links remain explicitly unsupported under that narrowest design.

Independent reconstruction: verifier commit 8889313c…e540 separately implements causal eligibility, risk-set counting, matched-set combinatorics, volatility ranks, farthest-first/merge partitioning, query reconstruction orchestration, cell assembly and expected HTML. It validates every frozen upstream digest, all packed bytes, every case seal, recurrent packed episode identity, all 3,270 causal stock/benchmark prefixes and every support count. All 12 integrity gates pass at semantic digest 1d14eabe…ca03; the one-file receipt SHA-256 is 62f805cb…f056. Its two runtime hashes and Git commit are recorded in the receipt. Twenty-eight focused tests cover parity, mutations, malformed JSON, self-provenance, one-cell semantics and no-overwrite publication.

Degenerate structure finding: K=8, K=12 and K=16 each retained exactly one structure cell after deterministic minimum-size merging. Every structure-conditioned support result is therefore identical to the base result. “Selected K=16” is only the preregistered requested-K tie-break and must never be described as 16 retained clusters. This is a valid negative engineering result, not corruption and not evidence for or against prediction.

Decision: N0 matching-design support is verified. N0 scientific execution remains blocked until its statistic and shared-priority contracts are frozen. N1/B2 remain blocked because N1 equals N0. The versioned replacement uses deterministic balanced recursive rank-PCA cells, remains blind to retrieval statistics and outcomes, and must pass exact cell-count, balance, reproducibility and support gates before any B2 statistic can be opened. No adequacy label, predictive claim, ranking change, production promotion or trading claim is authorized.

Reproduce: with a clean tree at verifier commit 8889313c…e540, run .venv/bin/python -m experiments.m04r.audit_m04r14_r1b_support_result --repository /home/vinay/code/market-analogues --workers 12 only into an absent create-only output root. Local producer report: R1-B v2 support. Replacement contract: N1 balanced partition.

L-293 — N1 v3 design tightened and synthetic partition kernel implemented

Why the plan changed before real data: independent design review found that “balanced PCA” still left numerical and inferential discretion: the rank formula, eigengap scale, fallback variance, projection boundary, solver threading, digests, failure states and K selection were not exact enough. No real v3 query partition or support value had been computed, so the contract was corrected before preregistration.

Frozen primary: K=8 is now the sole primary N1. K12/K16 are secondary sensitivities and cannot rescue it. This avoids selecting a non-nested K using observed retrieval membership and then treating the selected randomization test as automatically exact. K8 failure yields unresolved.

Leakage architecture: partition construction and support are separate publications. The partition executable will receive only the sealed query registry and causal representation inputs, never candidate identities, eligibility, selected membership, inbound counts, ranks, distances or outcomes. Only after the partition is sealed may the support executable open Qe/Ae. All failure modes publish a sealed status rather than leaving ambiguous absence.

Kernel: the new pure module uses persisted integer doubled-midranks; exact float64 rank mapping; locally centered scatter PCA under a one-thread BLAS limit; relative eigengap; exact integer-variance fallback; deterministic sign; fixed-order math.fsum projections; numerical boundary refusal; deterministic recursive allocation; and complete node audit fields. Raw float digests have explicit little-endian shape-prefixed encoding and are bounded to the sealed NumPy/LAPACK runtime.

Synthetic evidence only: 41 focused tests pass across the v2 verifier, existing support primitives and v3 kernel. V3 tests cover ties, constants, non-finite/noncanonical inputs, repeated eigenvalues, fallback feature choice, repeatability, no-overwrite semantics and exact 3,270-row leaf-size multisets: K8 = 6×409 + 2×408; K12 = 6×273 + 6×272; K16 = 6×205 + 10×204. This is implementation evidence, not a real-data result.

Next: implement both versioned executables and their failure artifacts, add synthetic mutation/differential tests, commit H0, then freeze sole-child H1 before either executable sees the real query vectors. No N1/B2 statistic, outcome, label, prediction or trading claim is authorized.
